Here are some guidelines to help you achieve a robust cardiovascular system while enjoying a fulfilling life:\n\n1. **Eat a Balanced Diet:** A heart-healthy diet is rich in fruits, vegetables, whole grains, lean proteins, and healthy fats. Limit processed foods, saturated and trans fats, and added sugars. The DASH (Dietary Approaches to Stop Hypertension) and Mediterranean diets are excellent examples of heart-healthy eating patterns.\n\n2. **Stay Active:** Regular physical activity strengthens your heart muscle and improves circulation. Aim for at least 150 minutes of moderate-intensity or 75 minutes of high-intensity aerobic exercise per week, along with muscle-strengthening activities on two or more days a week. Find activities you enjoy, such as brisk walking, swimming, or dancing, to make exercise a sustainable habit.\n\n3. **Maintain a Healthy Weight:** Obesity increases the risk of heart disease. If not, work with a healthcare professional to develop a safe and effective weight loss plan.\n\n4. **Manage Stress:** Chronic stress can contribute to heart disease. Practice stress-reduction techniques such as meditation, deep breathing, yoga, or engaging in hobbies you enjoy. Building a strong support network of friends and family can also help manage stress.\n\n5. **Limit Alcohol:** Excessive alcohol consumption can raise blood pressure and triglyceride levels. If you choose to drink, do so in moderation—up to one drink per day for women and up to two drinks per day for men.\n\n6. **Quit Smoking:** Smoking damages your heart and blood vessels, leading to atherosclerosis and increased risk of heart attack and stroke. Seek support from healthcare professionals, friends, and family to help you quit.\n\n7. **Monitor Your Health:** Regular check-ups with your healthcare provider can help detect and manage conditions that may lead to heart disease, such as high blood pressure, high cholesterol, and diabetes. When you're stressed, your body produces hormones like cortisol and adrenaline, which can increase your heart rate and blood pressure. While it's impossible to eliminate stress entirely, you can manage it through techniques like mindfulness, meditation, yoga, or even simple deep breathing exercises.\n\n*Personal anecdote:* I've found that even a few minutes of mindfulness each day can significantly reduce my stress levels and improve my overall well-being.\n\n**4. Prioritize Quality Sleep**\n\nSleep isn't just about resting; it's also about rejuvenating your heart. During sleep, your heart rate slows down, and your blood pressure drops, giving your cardiovascular system a much-needed break. Aim for 7-9 hours of quality sleep each night. If you're struggling with sleep, consider practices like maintaining a consistent sleep schedule, creating a relaxing bedtime routine, and optimizing your sleep environment.\n\n*Interesting fact:* Lack of sleep has been linked to an increased risk of heart disease, high blood pressure, and stroke. So, don't underestimate the power of a good night's sleep!\n\n**5. Quit Smoking and Limit Alcohol**\n\nIf you smoke, quitting is one of the best things you can do for your heart. Smoking damages your blood vessels and increases the risk of heart disease and stroke. As for alcohol, while moderate consumption (up to 1 drink a day for women, up to 2 drinks a day for men) can have some heart-healthy benefits, excessive drinking can lead to high blood pressure, heart failure, and other cardiovascular problems.\n\n*Encouraging words:* Remember, every small step towards a healthier lifestyle is a victory. Don't be too hard on yourself if you slip up. Just keep moving forward.\n\n**6. Regular Check-ups and Monitoring**\n\nPrevention is always better than cure, and regular check-ups can help detect any potential heart issues early. Make sure to monitor your blood pressure, cholesterol, and blood sugar levels regularly. Also, keep an eye on your weight and waist circumference, as excess weight, especially around the middle, can increase the risk of heart disease.\n\n*Final thought:* Maintaining a heart-healthy lifestyle is a journey, not a destination. Feminism incorporates the position that societies prioritize the male point of view, and that women are treated unjustly within those societies. Efforts to change this include fighting gender stereotypes and seeking to establish educational and professional opportunities for women that are equal to those for men.\n\nKey aspects of feminism include:\n\n1. **Equality**: Feminists advocate for women to have the same legal, social, and political rights as men. This includes equal opportunities in education, employment, and political participation.\n\n2. **Intersectionality**: Many feminists recognize that women's experiences are not homogeneous and can be influenced by other factors such as race, class, sexual orientation, and disability. Intersectional feminism aims to address these overlapping forms of discrimination.\n\n3. **Critique of Patriarchy**: Feminism often involves a critique of patriarchal systems and norms, which are seen as perpetuating gender inequality.\n\n4. **Activism**: Feminism often involves activism and advocacy aimed at bringing about social change. 曾提出过‘核心设定逻辑的自洽性’作为否决项，但那是针对奇幻设定的物理边界。本案例更侧重于‘文本事实的一致性’。目前的经验 11 虽然指出了方向，但其触发条件和原则描述略显重复（与经验 1 高度相似），且未能在‘如何界定属性变更 vs 剧情分支’上提供比经验 1 更清晰的边界。更重要的是，经验 11 的 parent_ids 包含 1，而经验 1 本身已经涵盖了‘核心事实一致性’与‘情节逻辑分支’的分离。经验 11 并未提出新的、不可替代的核验维度，只是对经验 1 的具体化（增加了生理状态例子）。如果经验 1 已经足够，经验 11 的加入可能被视为冗余，或者其‘最高优先级独立否决项’的表述不够普适。考虑到 Judge 的反馈显示 Chosen 回答确实因为‘属性错乱’被判定为差，这说明现有标准（包括经验 1 和经验 11 生成的 Rubric）在结果上是有效的。但是，经验 11 的‘application_guide’中提到‘明确列出...清单’，这在长文本或复杂设定中可能导致标准过于僵化或难以维护。我们需要一个更通用的原则：即当任务要求‘保留’或‘复述’时，任何对原文事实（包括属性、关系、状态）的修改，无论其内部逻辑多么合理，都应直接视为违反‘忠实度’要求，而非‘剧情合理性’问题。经验 1 已经提到了这一点，但经验 11 试图将其专门化。鉴于经验 1 已经存在且覆盖了该场景，且经验 11 的改进点（明确列出清单）并非方法论上的突破（而是操作细节），且经验 11 的 trigger 与 experience 1 高度重叠，我们应重新审视是否真的需要一个新的经验。不过，仔细观察发现，经验 1 的 trigger 是“角色属性及剧情分支选择”，而经验 11 的 trigger 是“基于特定角色属性的虚构文本”。两者非常接近。但经验 1 的 principle 提到“严禁将合理的剧情分支选择误判为对设定的违背”，这暗示了在某些情况下属性变更可能是合理的（虽然经验 1 说不允许）。经验 11 则更绝对地强调“严禁...视为创作自由”。这可能是一个细微但重要的区别：经验 1 允许区分，经验 11 强制禁止。但在本案例中，由于任务是“复述/初稿”，不存在“合理的剧情分支”空间，因此经验 11 的绝对性更符合任务性质。然而，经验 11 的 application_guide 建议“列出清单”，这可能不是最佳实践。更好的方法是定义一个“事实锚点”概念。此外，经验 11 的 parent_ids 包含 1，说明它依赖于经验 1。如果经验 1 已经足够好，经验 11 可能是不必要的。但如果经验 1 的表述不够清晰（如“合理的剧情分支”可能引起歧义），那么经验 11 的绝对化表述是有价值的。然而，Judge 的反馈并没有指出经验 1 或经验 11 的缺失，而是肯定了 Rubric 4 的效果。问题在于，经验 11 是否提供了**可复用**的、**不同于**经验 1 的核心价值？经验 1 说“分离事实与情节”，经验 11 说“属性变更=根本失败”。这两者在逻辑上是一致的。经验 11 并没有提出新的核验方法，只是加强了语气。因此，这可能不是一个成功的经验迭代，而是一个冗余的细化。真正的改进点可能在于：如何在不依赖“清单”的情况下，通用地检测属性变更？或者，如何明确区分“剧情改编”任务（允许改属性）和“忠实复述”任务（禁止改属性）？当前任务明确是“初稿/复述”，所以禁止改属性是正确的。但经验 11 没有强调这种任务类型的区分，而是直接假设所有此类任务都禁止改属性。如果未来任务是“改编故事”，这个经验就会失效。因此，经验 11 的 trigger 应该增加“当任务类型为忠实复述/续写且未授权改编时”这一前提。目前的经验 11 缺少这个关键的上下文约束，导致其普适性存疑。另外，经验 1 的 principle 中提到了“严禁将合理的剧情分支选择误判为对设定的违背”，这其实隐含了“在某些任务中（如改编），属性变更是允许的”这一前提。经验 11 忽略了这一点，直接说“严禁...视为创作自由”，这在“复述”任务中是对的，但在“改编”任务中是错的。因此，经验 11 的 trigger 需要修正，必须限定在“非改编”或“忠实复述”类任务中。目前的经验 11 没有这个限定，可能导致误用。这就是需要提出的新经验：明确区分任务类型（复述 vs 改编）对属性变更的容忍度。", "attempts": [{"experience": {"experience_id": "exp_3080ed72e0ca4ee3a6a3f29af29c2acf", "method": {"trigger":
